- **D-M. The command hook for D22's direct forms** (2026-11-18). A managed `PreToolUse` hook in the harness's requirements runs runtime-agent's own executable before every Codex command and refuses each direct launch the exec-policy rule misses, and a shell that would read the session's input (D22). What it reads, refuses and leaves to the accepted gap is in D22. It is on for every Codex turn on a Unix runtime, hosted ones included, as the rule is: a hook for Desktop alone would leave the same turns open on another runtime the person connects. Isolated tool workers keep hooks on, managed ones only. Windows gets the rule alone, since it has no lane (D18) and no test of the hook. The price is one short process before each command of every Codex turn, which the 2026-11-13 decision below deferred while the lane was dark.

Also decided that day, while fixing what the review of the build found:
- The D22 rule ships as it is, with no per-command hook yet: a hook would start a process before every Codex command of every turn, now, for a lane that is dark. Closing the forms it misses is a phase 8 gate (D22, section 9). The hook followed on 2026-11-18, as phase 8 work (D-M).
- A teammate's Details for a failed turn keep the steps, not the raw text (5.5).
- The startup probe has one deadline for its three commands (3.2).
- The lane's shared lists and one recorded lane job payload live in runtime-contracts, and every package tests against them (2.6, 8).
- Studio words `claude_code_runtime_not_ready` by its reason (5.5).
- Two behaviours are documented, not changed: teammates get a lane turn's streaming reply draft, as for any turn on `main` (5.7), and Desktop trusts Studio's session as the key of the local consent record (4.1).

---

## Appendix A. As built

Where the build chose a detail the design left open, or checked more than it asked. D-K accepts each. Sections 1-10 stay the reference; this list helps read the code.

### A.1 Controller

- **Intake.** The claim is read in both spellings. The controller's own keys (`claudeCode`, `claudeCodeFailure`, in both spellings) are stripped from a send's metadata and from its nested copies (`prompt_metadata`, `promptMetadata`, `details`). The person's message carries the lane stamps at the top of its metadata and inside `prompt_metadata`.
- **Stage 1 order.** G1; then G2's service-role, own-session and delegation checks; then every G3 key; then the rest of G2 (typed, received now, an existing chat). All of these are 403 `claude_code_turn_not_person`, so the order changes only `details.reason`.
- **G3 keys.** Wider than 2.2's list, failing closed: also `retryAutomatic`, `dispatch_content`, `browserHandBack` and `browser_hand_back`, `space_review`, and `browserRequestOf`, a browser card's continuation, which asks in Instafy's words. A key counts when present, null included, and its refusal names it in `details.key`.
- **G5 and G6.** `ui.requestedPreview: false` is refused too, since 2.2 asks for `requested_preview` to be absent. G6 also requires the canonical `_instafySelfHostedAccess` with `mode: "private"` and an `ownerUserId` equal to the sender, as the lease clause compares it, and compares the capability's version as a number, so `1.0` counts and the string `"1"` does not. The runtime row is loaded `for share` in Stage 2's `admit`, not where the Personal Browser check runs, and is then the dispatch's strict runtime record.
- **Payload and pin.** A lane payload that would carry the evaluation marker is a 500 that rolls back, which the gate makes unreachable. `target_runtime_for_agent_job` is unchanged; dispatch pins the job to `admission.runtime_id` where it calls it. `AgentJob` serializes `engine` only when it is set, so other jobs' lease answers are unchanged.
- **Reasons** (`details.reason`). G1: `not_allowed`. G2: `service_role`, `not_own_session`, `delegated`, `not_typed`, `received_earlier`, `new_conversation`. G3: `queued`, `goal_wakeup`, `goal_parent_job`, `automation`, `internal_review`, `site_intent`, `hand_back`, `automatic_retry`, `thread_kind`, `resend`, `goal_continuation`, `dispatch_content`, `display_content`, `special_dispatch`, `controller_dispatch`, `space_review`, `browser_card`. G4 and G5: `not_plain_turn`, `targets`, `not_octo`, `recorded_earlier`, `intent`, `execution_mode`, `plan_seed`, `tool_limits`, `repo`, `requested_preview`, `thread_kind`, `attachments`, `empty`, `too_long`, `command`, `write_intent`, `write_scope`. G6: `no_runtime`, `runtime_missing`, `not_private`, `not_owner`, `personal_browser`, `not_dispatch_ready`, `capability_missing`, `capability_not_ready`. G7: `machine_busy`. G8: `turn_active`, `start_window_passed`. Granted chat: `conversation_granted`. Unknown claim: `unknown_engine`. Steer: `steer`. Secrets, job token and proxy renewal: `local_engine`. A lane job's refused message: `plan`, `user_input_request`, `hand_back`, `action_request`, and `too_deep` for metadata nested past 12 levels.
- **A lane job's messages.** They are refused by message type (`multi_agent_plan`, `action_request`, `user_input_request`, `request_browser`) and by plan, question, browser-card and hand-back keys at any depth; past 12 levels the walk fails closed. A lane parent's plan gets a 403 rather than the browser-bound 400, so the job is never settled as a refused plan.
- **Runtime token.** Naming `claude_code.turns` without `localEngine: "claude-code"` is a 400, as for `personal_browser.control`; naming it with the flag but without the right to it is a 403. Sending `localEngine` without that right gets a token without the scope and no error, so Desktop's start works while the switch is empty. Any other `localEngine` value asks for nothing.
- **Attestation and status.** Besides the Personal Browser condition of 2.10, attestation strips a snake_case `claude_code`, keeps `reason` only while `ready` is false, and accepts `cli` as MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH with an optional short suffix. Status's viewer is a person's own interactive session, so a scoped token or the service role sees neither `claudeCode` nor `claudeCodeLane`.
- **Completion.** A lane conversation state is kept only when it names the runtime that leased the job, rebuilt from its known fields, and a state with extra keys is dropped; `load_session` also needs its `runtimeId` to be the admitted runtime. Codex run logs are dropped by a second update in the completion's transaction, and the planning-receipt path also drops a provider or state that names `claude-code`. The usage row is a `token_usage` message whose details are `{kind: "claude_code_turn_usage", billed: false, model, usage}`, with the model cut to 200 characters and the four raw counts.
- **Sweeps and stop.** The expired-lease step treats a leased lane job with no `lease_expires_at` as expired, as the idle release does, and locks rows in id order. A stop's failure message is stored as "Instafy Desktop stopped on this turn's computer, so the turn ended and won't run again. Send it again to run it.", and Studio words it from `claudeCodeFailure`. The stopped and quarantined runtime events list the lane jobs they failed. `record_job_end` reads only jobs with no engine, so a lane job teaches pacing nothing.
- **Guard.** `claudeAiOauth` and `claude_ai_oauth` match ignoring ASCII case, and the JSON walk checks object keys as well as strings. The letter or digit before `sk-ant-` is ASCII only. A value at depth 9 or deeper counts as material. A refused decode answers with the same 400 `claude_credential_refused`. `refused` is sent only when true, by the listing and by an update. The listing judges a row that no key opens by its name alone, and delivery skips a reserved name before decrypting it.

### A.2 Runtime agent

- **Capture.** A missing or empty `INSTAFY_CLAUDE_CODE_BIN`, a path that does not exist, one that is not a regular file and one with no execute bit give `binary_missing`. A relative path, a bad user id, a malformed `INSTAFY_CLAUDE_CODE_ENV_JSON`, a relative D26 path, a failed ownership, writability or folder check, and too many links give `binary_unsafe`. The capture runs the file checks, and a failure publishes the not-ready capability at once, with no probe. PATH entries are checked each time the environment is built. The `admin` allowance comes from the `admin` group on macOS only, and D26 values get none.
- **Probe.** The 256 KiB cap counts stdout and stderr together. A per-turn `auth status` that fails gives `planSignIn: false`, never a failed probe; only at startup does one that the shared deadline cuts short fail the probe (3.2). Probe folders and the MCP config file live in the launch snapshot's `TMPDIR`, mode 0700, and a `TMPDIR` inside the workspace roots fails the probe. Since 2026-11-19 `CLAUDE_CODE_LANE_VERIFIED_THROUGH` and the floor are both 2.1.325, the version Phase 0 ran on (B.4). So 2.1.325 reports `verified: true`, though the live suite has not yet run on it, and any newer version reports `verified: false` until a reviewed change raises the constant after the live suite passes on that version.
- **Events.** The adapter sends a `draft.reset` event of its own after top-level commentary, and only the lane sends it, so Codex's drafts are unchanged. Commentary is recognized from the start of a `tool_use` block. A `result` whose subtype starts with `error` counts as an error. An exit counts as 0 only when it is 0, 143 after the runtime's own SIGTERM, or 1 after a last `result` that was an error while the turn waits only for follow-up turns (3.6), so a `claude` that must be killed after its result fails as `crashed`. Work before `system/init`, or a success with no `init`, is `unexpected_configuration`; exit 0 with no result is `crashed`. A `result` before `init` is not work: after `--resume`, an error `result` whose `errors` name the missing session is `session_unavailable` whatever the exit status (2.7). The fallbacks that classify from text are fixed phrase lists, for a sign-in, a plan limit and a resume miss, and the plan-limit reset time goes in the failure text as "The limit resets at <UTC time>." The run log leaves out `stream_event` lines and thinking; from every line it keeps, it drops each key whose name contains `cost` or `credential`, in any letter case and at any depth (`total_cost_usd`, `costUSD`, `costBasis`, `credential_expires_at`); it drops a `result`'s `modelUsage` and `model_usage_since_last_result` whole; and of a `rate_limit_event` it keeps only `type`, `uuid`, `session_id` and the `status` and `resetsAt` of its `rate_limit_info` (3.6). The quiet label reads "Looking through files" ("looked through files" once done), for Read, Grep, Glob, LS and NotebookRead.
- **Pre-flight.** Each `preflight_failed` refusal says why in its first line: "Instafy took too long to check this space's folder." past the deadline; "git took too long to list this space's files, so Instafy couldn't check them." after git's 30 s; "git couldn't list this space's files, so Instafy couldn't check them: …", with git's first line cut to 300 characters before the turn's redaction (3.6); "… is too large for Instafy to check what it brings in." over 1 MiB; "… has too many @ imports for Instafy to check what it brings in." over the reading budget; "… has an @ import too long for Instafy to check what it brings in." for a word past 4,096 characters; "This space's CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md files bring in too many files for Instafy to check." past 512 files; and "This space's CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md files have too many @ imports for Instafy to check what they bring in." past 20,000 candidates. When the folder or a memory file cannot be read, or git cannot be run, the line says so with the system's own error, through the same redaction.
- **Job checks.** Markers are refused by key presence with any value but null: 3.9's list, plus `spaceReview`, `controllerDispatch`, `workerInputRouting`, `multi_agent_plan`, the browser hand-back keys, `dispatch_content`, the payload's `plan_seed`, `planSeed` and `browser_hand_back_reads_note`, a group participation of `agent_evaluation`, and attachments under any of three keys.
- **Turn.** A failed turn sends no conversation state, except the reset after `session_unavailable`. A file whose content cannot be read stays off the save, named "Not saved: … (Instafy couldn't check it for a Claude key)". A link is checked by the path it holds, which is what git saves. Held pages, the origin's not-saved sentences, the lane filter and the stayed-on-this-computer line are each a note of their own.
- **Re-registration** is wired in agent.rs: each registration sends a copy of the configuration with the lane's published capability, and the lease loop adopts a renewal at its next idle point, which restarts the origin and tunnel as a token renewal already does.
- **D22.** Where Codex names a requirement's source, the rule's source reads "enterprise-managed requirements Instafy runtime (instafy-runtime-agent)". The three loaders build from one `runtime_codex_config_builder`, and the fallback is a named `load_fallback_codex_config`. The command hook (D-M) is in the same requirement: matcher `Bash`, the command `'<runtime-agent's executable>' --codex-claude-guard`, single-quoted for the shell Codex runs hooks in, and a 30 s timeout. A runtime whose executable's path cannot be named, or is not UTF-8, builds no Codex config at all, so it runs no Codex turn without the hook. An integration test whose process is not runtime-agent's executable names the executable with `use_claude_guard_program`.
- **The live suite** is an ignored test whose reason starts "not run:". Run with `--ignored` but without its variable, it fails, and it refuses to run when `CI` is set. Without `INSTAFY_CLAUDE_CODE_BIN` it runs the first `claude` on `PATH`. An item the model did not show is marked unconfirmed, which fails the suite.

### A.3 Desktop

- **Ids.** The feature module is `instafy.claude-code`; its extension and launch grant are `claude-code`.
- **Host context.** It also carries native dialogs, with a file picker that keeps a picked link as a link, and the folders Desktop owns, for validation. The consent store is scoped to the module, the controller origin and the user, so a method reaches no other record, and a grant's `resolve` receives that record.
- **Controller origin.** The invoke request may carry `controllerUrl`, which the runtime-start trust policy checks. Without it, a packaged build uses its pinned controller and an unpackaged one its running runtime's.
- **Detection** prints PATH and the D26 values with `/usr/bin/printenv`, so they are the exported values in any shell, fish included, between markers that carry a nonce for each run. On a timeout the shell's whole process group is killed.
- **Grant keys** must start with `INSTAFY_`, and never with `INSTAFY_RUNTIME_` or `INSTAFY_PERSONAL_BROWSER_`. Both the registry, when it is built, and desktop-runtime-agent, on every start, check this, so a grant can never override the runtime's identity, controller or proxy values.

### A.4 Studio

- **The lane flag.** The bridge reads `claudeCodeLane` with a runtime-status request of its own, and only after Desktop's module answers `supported: true`: on mount, when the space changes, and on window focus at most every 5 minutes. An entry's `claudeCode` is kept only when the same answer carries `claudeCodeLane: true`. After a refused `status`, a page asks Desktop no more; a refused action does not stop status reads.
- **Desktop.** Studio invokes the extension `claude-code` and passes `controllerUrl`.
- **Sends.** A claimed send skips the whole send-time title call, the local first-line name included, so a chat with only lane messages keeps its default title. Beyond 5.2's list, a message over 100,000 characters is blocked in Studio, the private-chat invite check runs before routing, and the preflight skips its `/invite` and GitHub-import handling for a claimed send and never queues one once Studio's own busy check has passed. A routed send's metadata carries the participation bypass, never a participation decision. The claim helpers read both spellings and remove the key whatever its value.
- **Wording.** For a lane turn, the starting line takes the place of the notice that a cloud computer is busy. A lane failure's own first line is shown only when it also avoids "runtime", "switch" and ids. A `plan_limit` reset time is read only from text such as "resets 2pm", "reset at 5pm (TZ)" or "resets in 3 hours".
- **Your AI row.** It reads the lane flag and readiness for the space Desktop runs on this computer or, when none runs, the space open in Studio. The first-use note shows once the lane is on, except while this computer's runtime reports a Claude plan sign-in or the row checks a runtime Desktop just started, and its Got it is remembered per browser. Find and Locate… always show, and Use for my messages waits until Claude Code is found.

### A.5 Checked end to end

On 2026-11-13 the built controller and runtime ran against an isolated local stack, with the runtime started as Desktop starts it and the fake binary in place of `claude`. A covered send was admitted and ran once, with 3.3's exact argv, the allowlisted environment and the prompt on stdin byte for byte. It streamed its steps, stamped its reply and saved its session, and the next message resumed it. A second send while it ran got `claude_code_machine_busy`, a steer got `claude_code_turn_takes_no_input`, and a Stop killed the turn's background child and its `setsid` grandchild. Signed-out and plan-limit runs failed with their codes. An old version failed its turn as `unsupported_version`; the runtime registered again as not ready, the next send got `claude_code_runtime_not_ready`, and once the version was restored, the timed re-probe made the lane ready again in about 50 s. With the switch empty, as shipped, nothing was attested, the status route said nothing of the lane, and a claimed send got 403 `claude_code_turn_not_allowed` and recorded nothing. The drive found two defects, both fixed: the runtime read the payload's top-level `executionMode` as the execution mode (2.5, 3.9), and Studio worded every `claude_code_runtime_not_ready` as a computer that isn't connected (5.5).

On 2026-11-16, at the maintainer's request, Instafy Desktop was also driven once with the maintainer's own signed-in `claude` in place of the fake binary, on the build of that morning, and one lane turn ran end to end. Its output is not part of Phase 0's record, and it confirms none of the Phase 0 items (B.1).

---

## Appendix B. Phase 0 results

Phase 0 (section 9) ran on 2026-11-16. This appendix records how it ran, each item's verdict with its evidence, what it changed in this design and what stays open before phase 8. The output is a real person's, so it stays with the maintainer, outside the repository, and nothing here quotes it: evidence is cited by run name and line number.

### B.1 What ran, and how

- **Who ran it.** At the maintainer's explicit request, an agent drove the Phase 0 runbook, section 9's written script, from a Claude Code session's shell on the maintainer's Mac and login. A driver script sourced the runbook's blocks as written, after unsetting the session's own `CLAUDE*` and `ANTHROPIC*` variables: the set-up blocks and the probe (02-05), then blocks 06-20 back to back in one non-interactive shell, in about 100 s, then the clean-up (21). The blocks it sourced match the runbook byte for byte.
- **Which `claude`.** The maintainer's own signed-in Claude Code CLI 2.1.325, a native install, the same version at the start and at the end (02.log line 2; 21-cleanup.log line 1). A first attempt found another copy of `claude` on the session's PATH, which the probe found with no Claude plan sign-in; it stopped at the probe, made no model request and was discarded. The runs cited here are the second attempt.
- **How it departs from section 9.**
  - Not by hand. An agent ran `claude`, and in blocks 19 and 21 it listed and searched files under the person's Claude Code project folder, as the runbook's steps 11 and 13 do. Section 9 says no agent runs `claude` or reads `~/.claude`; the maintainer's request is what allowed it here.
  - Not on the floor version of the time: 2.1.325, not 2.1.314. The floor rose to 2.1.325 on 2026-11-19 (B.4).
  - No terminal. Every `claude` ran without a controlling terminal and in one process group, unlike blocks pasted into Terminal and closer to how runtime-agent starts it, so the runbook's notes about Terminal do not apply.
  - One unattended loop ran every block, so nothing stopped at the finding in block 12, where the runbook says to stop (item 5).
  - An allow flag the lane never passes. Step 7's two runs, `bash-end` and `bash-wait`, added `--allowedTools 'Bash(sleep:*)'`, which is on 3.3's never-passed list, so that their `sleep` commands could run at all: under the lane's flags a Bash command no allow rule permits is declined. It changed nothing in the person's settings. In a lane turn such commands run only if the person's own allow rules permit them; otherwise `--permission-prompts none` declines them, and none of the processes, exit statuses and follow-up turns that items 5, 6 and 9 cite from those two runs would exist. What the runs show of a command that does run stands.
  - Skipped: block 16, item 8's route through a test OS account, and item 13, which needs Desktop.
- **The harness is the lane, except where a step changes it.** The runbook's harness starts `claude` with 3.3's argv, element for element, and 3.4's environment, which held exactly `ENABLE_CLAUDEAI_MCP_SERVERS`, `HOME`, `LOGNAME`, `PATH`, `SHELL`, `TMPDIR` and `USER` (for example 06.log line 4), with no `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, so the person's own user settings loaded. Some runs changed that on purpose, as their steps say: `mcp-plain`, `mcp-var-only` and `mcp-strict-only` left out the strict MCP flags, the variable or both (step 4); `bash-end` and `bash-wait` added an allow flag (step 7, above); `unknown-flag` added a made-up flag (step 9); and `probe-config-dir` and `signed-out` set `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` to an empty, throwaway folder, so the person's own settings did not load there (step 10). The harness gives `claude` the prompt on stdin and closes it, and either stops `claude` at its first top-level `result`, as the lane first did, lets it exit by itself, or stops it at the point a check needs. It differs from a lane turn chiefly in that its PATH was the shell's, unfiltered, its folders were fresh repositories in the temporary folder rather than Desktop folders with the scaffold, and it read Claude Code's output itself rather than through runtime-agent. The check found the run faithful to the runbook's steps.
- **The output.** The maintainer keeps it, outside the repository: a log for each runbook block (`NN.log`), and for each run its stream-json, a summary, its stderr and the processes it started, sampled every 200 ms. Below, a run's name with a line number cites its stream-json ("background-agent line 49"), "summary" its summary block, and `NN.log` the log of block NN. The runs are `baseline` and `resume-hit` (step 2), `isolation` (step 3), `mcp-plain`, `mcp-var-only`, `mcp-strict-only` and `mcp-lane` (step 4), `edits` (step 5), `background-agent` (step 6), `bash-end` and `bash-wait` (step 7), `sigterm`, `sigint` and `epipe` (step 8), `resume-miss` and `unknown-flag` (step 9), `probe-config-dir` and `signed-out` (step 10), and `memory-write` and `memory-read` (step 11), beside the probe (step 1).
- **The check.** An independent check then read the output against this design and the runtime's code, item by item, and verified each of its findings a second time. Where a verdict rests on a reading of Claude Code's own code rather than on a run, B.2 says so; that reading looked at the installed program as a file and never ran it.
- **Desktop.** Separately, the maintainer authorized one drive of Instafy Desktop with their own signed-in `claude`, on the build of that morning, and one lane turn ran end to end (A.5). It is not part of this record and confirms none of the Phase 0 items: not item 13, which stays open (B.4), and not item 5's rule as now built.

### B.2 Verdicts

Every verdict is for 2.1.325 alone.

| Item | Verdict |
|---|---|
| 1. The flags and the `--bare` wording | holds with caveats |
| 2. Project configuration stays out | holds with caveats; three parts not checked |
| 3. Strict, empty MCP | partly checked |
| 4. The prompt on stdin | holds |
| 5. The stream-json shapes | holds, with caveats for the task events and the `result` fields; the end-of-turn rule as first built fails, and the rule as now built has not run on a real `claude` |
| 6. SIGTERM, SIGINT and a closed stdout | holds with caveats |
| 7. A resume of a missing session | holds |
| 8. The signed-out and plan-limit texts | partly checked |
| 9. The Bash tool's process groups | holds with caveats (recorded; no pass or fail) |
| 10. What a `-p` run writes into the folder | holds |
| 11. Edits outside the folder | holds with caveats |
| 12. External imports | holds with caveats |
| 13. A keychain prompt under Desktop | not checked |
| 14. Auto memory | partly checked |

- **Item 1, the flags and the `--bare` wording: holds with caveats.** The probe (05-probe.log lines 2-30) ran `--version` and `--help`, and both exited 0. The `--bare` entry (lines 6-24) lists what minimal mode skips and never says it is the default, so the bare check passes, and the runtime's own reading of that help agrees; there is no `--no-bare` (line 25); `--help` lists every lane flag (line 28). `baseline` ran 3.3's exact argv (06.log line 2) and exited 0 with no unknown-option text (06.log lines 5 and 21), its `init` first and its `result` a success (baseline lines 1 and 13). `resume-hit` added `--resume`, and its `init` kept that session (07.log lines 2 and 11). As a control, `unknown-flag` failed at startup, exit 1 with no `init` (unknown-flag summary lines 5-6), so an accepted flag means something. Caveats: `--no-bare`'s path is untested; the probe's timings were not recorded, so whether its three commands fit the startup's one 10 s deadline is unknown; the help parser is pinned only by synthetic fixtures, not by 2.1.325's own text.
- **Item 2, project configuration stays out: holds with caveats, with three parts not checked.** `isolation` ran the lane's argv in a folder planted with every kind of project configuration, tracked in its repository (04.log lines 1-12), below a parent folder with a `CLAUDE.md` of its own. With tools forbidden, the model was asked to list every canary token it could see; it used no tool and listed only the prompt's own (isolation lines 17 and 22; 09.log lines 14-20).
  - `.claude/settings.json` (hooks, `env` with `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `apiKeyHelper`, an output style): holds with caveats. No hook or helper left its marker (09.log line 31); `init` reported `apiKeySource` none and the default output style, as `baseline` did (isolation line 1); the request went through although the planted base URL named a port where nothing listens (08.log line 1; isolation line 22); and the reply did not open with the planted style's text (isolation line 17).
  - `.claude/settings.local.json` (hooks, `env`): holds with caveats. Neither hook left a marker (09.log line 31); its `env` value had no channel the run could read.
  - `enabledPlugins`: partly checked. The planted entry names a plugin that does not exist, so it could not show either way; `init`'s plugins equal `baseline`'s.
  - Skills, agents, commands and output styles: holds with caveats. `init`'s skills, agents, slash commands, tools and plugins are the same sets as in `baseline`, whose folder had nothing planted (isolation line 1 against baseline line 1), and no `commands_changed` line followed.
  - Hooks in skill and agent frontmatter: holds with caveats. No marker; the skill and the agent never registered, so their hooks could not run.
  - The project `CLAUDE.md` in the folder and its parent, its imports, `.claude/rules/` and `CLAUDE.local.md`: holds with caveats. None of their tokens was listed. Apart from the model, the first request's input (isolation line 10 against baseline line 8) exceeds `baseline`'s by about what the longer prompt adds, which leaves no room for even one planted memory file with the header Claude Code puts before each.
  - `AGENTS.md`, at whatever Project instructions value the run had: holds with caveats. Its token was not listed, and the same count applies. That value was not recorded, and a space with `AGENTS.md` and no `CLAUDE.md`, this repository's own shape, was not tried. A reading of 2.1.325's code found that `AGENTS.md` loads only through the built-in plugin `cc-plugin-agents-md`, listed in every `init`, the signed-out run's included, whose lookups the project setting source gates (7.5).
  - The `claude-md-and-agents-md` variant of Project instructions: not checked.
  - Files that Claude Code loads on demand below the folder's root: not checked. Tools were forbidden, and nothing was planted below the root.
  - Item 2 on the 2.1.314 floor: not checked. Moot since the floor rose to 2.1.325 on 2026-11-19 (B.4).

- **Item 3, strict and empty MCP: partly checked.** `mcp-plain`, with neither the strict flags nor the variable, listed one server, a claude.ai connector (10.log line 6). `mcp-var-only`, with the variable alone, listed none (10.log line 32), and its other counts were unchanged (line 33 against line 7): `ENABLE_CLAUDEAI_MCP_SERVERS=false` removes exactly that connector. `mcp-strict-only`, with the flags alone, listed none (10.log line 58), and so did `mcp-lane` (10.log line 84). The root `.mcp.json` planted for `isolation` did not load (09.log lines 6 and 31). Every `init` with the lane's flags lists no server. Not checked: servers of the person's own and plugin servers, since this setup had neither; `mcp-plain`'s four plugins are built-in and start no server. The `.mcp.json` result does not isolate `--strict-mcp-config`, since the project source was off too; the pre-flight refuses a root `.mcp.json` anyway (3.9).
- **Item 4, the prompt on stdin: holds.** `baseline` sent a two-line prompt on stdin only, and the reply was exactly the token on its second line (06.log lines 2-3, 18 and 20; baseline line 13). `resume-hit`, `isolation` and `edits` also followed prompts given only on stdin (07.log line 19; 09.log line 18; 11.log line 20). D30's fallback is not needed. Only short prompts were tried.
- **Item 5, the stream-json shapes.**
  - The `init` fields: holds. All 19 `init` lines, in 17 runs, carry `session_id`, `cwd`, `model`, `permissionMode` and `mcp_servers`, and every one with the lane's argv passes the launch checks. A follow-up turn's second `init` (background-agent line 38; bash-wait line 46) repeats the first apart from its `uuid`. Every `init` also carries `original_cwd`, equal to its `cwd` since no run moved the shell, which the launch check of a later `init` now reads beside its `cwd` (3.6).
  - `stream_event` text deltas, content blocks and `parent_tool_use_id`: holds. Every `stream_event` line carries `parent_tool_use_id`, null; text arrives as `text_delta`; blocks start as text, `tool_use` or thinking. The background subagent printed no `stream_event`, only an `assistant` line with `parent_tool_use_id` set (background-agent line 34). Every top-level `message_start` came after a `system/status` `requesting` line. With streaming tool execution, a request's own `message_stop`, and in some requests its `message_delta`, could come after its tool result (background-agent lines 19-21; bash-wait lines 31-33), though in other requests both came before it (bash-wait lines 12-13 and 17).
  - The task events: holds with caveats. `task_started` carries `task_id` and `task_type`, `local_agent` for the subagent (background-agent line 18) and `local_bash` for a command (bash-end lines 15 and 30), and two keys the design did not name, `run_id` and `is_backgrounded`. `task_notification` carries `task_id` and an ended status, and no `task_type` (background-agent line 36; bash-end lines 16 and 43; bash-wait lines 16 and 44). `background_tasks_changed` lists its tasks under `tasks`, each with a `task_id` (background-agent lines 17 and 37; bash-end and bash-wait lines 29 and 45). A command in the foreground gets a started and notification pair too. 2.1.325 also prints a `task_updated` line, which always came beside an ended `task_notification`. Caveats: no line marked a task `ambient`, so that rule is unverified; no subagent started a task, so no run showed such a task, which the adapter awaits as any other (3.6); there is one subagent sample; Workflow, Monitor and foreground `Agent` tasks were not exercised.
  - The `result` fields: holds with caveats. `result`, `is_error`, `usage`, `permission_denials` and `total_cost_usd` are on every successful `result`. An error `result` has `errors` and no `result` (resume-miss line 1; sigint line 11), and signed-out line 4 is of subtype success with `is_error` true. Caveats: `usage` covers only its own `result`, as `background-agent` and `bash-wait` show, and by a reading of 2.1.325's code so does `permission_denials`, so a turn with several results must combine them (2.6, 3.7); and 2.1.325's results carry keys the design did not name, `result_index`, `origin`, `model_usage_since_last_result` and `credential_expires_at` among them, and the run log as first built kept a cost estimate and the sign-in's expiry through them (3.6).
  - Shapes the design did not name: holds. Lines and keys the adapter does not know (`task_updated`, `commands_changed`, `status`, `thinking_tokens` and `init`'s other extra keys) are ignored and trip no launch check. The adapter as now built reads two that it first ignored: it counts each `system/permission_denied` line as a denial (3.7), and checks a later `init` by its `cwd` and its `original_cwd` (3.6). The only line before an `init` in any run is resume-miss line 1, a `result`, which is not work.
  - The end-of-turn rule as first built: fails. Replayed on every run's output, it ended `background-agent` too early. There the subagent's notification (line 36) and an empty task list (line 37) came before a second `init` (line 38) and a follow-up turn, and then two results arrived together: the main turn's, an interim sentence (line 49), and the follow-up's, the subagent's answer, with `origin.kind` `task-notification` (line 50). The rule ended at line 49 and replied with the interim sentence. In `bash-wait` the first `result` (line 42) came while only the background command ran, and the command's follow-up came later (`init` line 46, `result` line 62). Ending only once every top-level `init` has its `result` changes `background-agent` alone, which then ends at line 50 with the answer, and ends every other run where it ended before. The check gave that rule holds with caveats, since the runs did not show a subagent's notification before the first `result` with its follow-up's `init` after it, a background command that ends before the first `result`, a foreground `Agent`, or an `init` that never gets its `result`. 3.6 has the rule as it now stands, and replayed the same way it ends every run where that rule does. It also covers those cases, and others that later readings of 2.1.325's code found and no run showed: a notification folded into the running turn, which prints nothing and which the lane does not try to detect, so its task stays awaited; a tool step that ends the turn and folds nothing in; a task a subagent started, which is awaited as any other, and a subagent that ends while such a task runs; the follow-up wait, for a notification that was folded in or went to the subagent that started its task, with its restarts after other turns in between, including turns that start tasks of their own; a Monitor watch's event turn, which is never the reply, and a task that such a turn starts, whose follow-up can be; a notification run as a held turn, whose `result` is never the reply and whose run waits for the turn that answers it; and `claude`'s own exit once nothing else runs, which ends the turn as the follow-up wait would when it is 0, or 1 after a last `result` that was an error. Section 8's tests replay them, from orders built by hand. The rule as now built has not run on a real `claude`: B.4 has that run, and its result goes here.
- **Item 6, SIGTERM, SIGINT and a closed stdout: holds with caveats.** SIGTERM mid-turn: `claude` exited with status 143, not killed by the signal, and printed no `result` after it (sigterm summary line 5). At the end-of-turn SIGTERM after a `result`, it exited 0 in `baseline`, `resume-hit`, `isolation`, `edits`, `memory-write` and `memory-read`, and 143 in `bash-end`, where Claude Code stopped a running background command (bash-end line 43), and in `signed-out`; the runtime counts both as 0 after its own SIGTERM. SIGINT to the `claude` pid alone: exit 0, after an error `result` (sigint line 11). In the lane only Desktop's group SIGINT reaches `claude`, and the turn then ends as a shutdown that reports nothing (3.6). A stdout closed right after `init`: `claude` neither stopped nor failed, and exited 0 (epipe summary line 5). Caveats: one run each; no SIGTERM during a foreground command; Desktop's group SIGINT, and whether `claude` stops its commands on SIGINT, untested.
- **Item 7, a resume of a missing session: holds.** `claude` exited 1 about a second in, with no `init` (resume-miss summary lines 5-6). Its only stdout line is a top-level error `result`, before any `init`, whose `errors` name the missing session (resume-miss line 1), and its stderr says the same (resume-miss stderr line 1). It ran git in the folder first (resume-miss processes lines 1-4). The control, `unknown-flag`, also exits 1, with nothing on stdout and other text on stderr: the exit status does not tell the two apart, and the text does. The lane's SIGTERM at that `result` races the exit, which is why the runtime reads the miss from the structured `result` (2.7).
- **Item 8, the signed-out and plan-limit texts: partly checked.** Only the second route ran, an empty, throwaway `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` in the maintainer's own account; the route through a test OS account (block 16) did not. `auth status` with that folder exits 1 with `authMethod` none (probe-config-dir summary line 29), which gives `planSignIn: false`. `signed-out`'s `init` passes the launch checks, and an `assistant` line with `error` `authentication_failed` (line 3) and a `result` of subtype success with `is_error` true (line 4) classify as `not_signed_in`. Not seen: a plan limit, since no `rate_limit_event` in any run was `rejected`; a never-configured account; an expired or revoked sign-in.
- **Item 9, the Bash tool's process groups: holds with caveats.** Item 9 has no pass or fail; this is its record. Every command, in the foreground and in the background, ran in a shell that is `claude`'s child and leads a session and process group of its own, with the command its child in that group (bash-end summary lines 30-33; bash-wait summary lines 32-35). Only `claude`'s own helpers, a shell for each Bash run and git, stayed in its group. A background command keeps its shell as its parent, so it is not 1.2's escape, and at the end-of-turn SIGTERM Claude Code stopped it itself (bash-end line 43), leaving nothing running (bash-end summary line 35). Against the kill path: the tracker follows parent links whatever the session, but Desktop's group stop and its proof never cover a command (1.2, 3.6). Not tried: a command that puts a process in the background inside its own script and exits; SIGTERM during a foreground command; SIGINT while commands run.
- **Item 10, what a `-p` run writes into the folder: holds.** No run changed anything in the folder outside `.git`, under `.claude/` or inside `.git`, beyond the one file `edits` asked for (11.log lines 27-28). That includes `baseline` (06.log lines 26-28) and `isolation`, whose folder already held a `.claude/` (09.log lines 26-28). The declined write to `.claude/` left nothing behind (11.log line 34). What Claude Code keeps for itself, its session records and auto memory, went to the person's Claude Code project folder, and its messaging socket to the temporary folder. Caveats: fresh repositories, not Desktop folders with the scaffold and `.instafy/.git`; tools that make `.claude/` entries on purpose, such as a worktree, not exercised (3.9); files made and removed within a run are invisible to the before-and-after walk.
- **Item 11, edits outside the folder: holds with caveats.** `edits` asked for four writes. Claude Code declined three without a prompt, each with a `system/permission_denied` line and an error tool result: one in a folder in the home folder and one in a folder beside the space's, as outside the allowed working directories (edits lines 26-27 and 39-40), and one under the folder's `.claude/`, as a sensitive file (lines 65-66). The file inside the folder was written (line 70), the `result` lists the three denials (line 138), and nothing was written outside (11.log lines 32-35). The precondition: the person's settings add no directory. Every `init` reported `additional_directories: []`, and both outside declines came from the working-directory check, so a directory the person's settings add would be edited without asking (3.3). Claude Code's auto memory is a second exception (item 14). Only `Write` was tried: not `Edit`, `NotebookEdit` or the Bash file commands, and not `.git` or a link that points out.
- **Item 12, external imports: holds with caveats.** The planted `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` each imported a file in the home folder and a file by absolute path. `isolation` listed neither import's token, nor any token of the files that held them (isolation lines 17 and 22), so the host files did not load, and their imports had nothing to load from. No positive control shows these imports loading with the project source on, and the `claude-md-and-agents-md` variant was not run. In a real space the pre-flight refuses such imports first (`unsafe_import`, 3.9).
- **Item 13, a keychain prompt under Desktop: not checked.** No Phase 0 run was started by Desktop's runtime. The runs did read Claude Code's own claude.ai sign-in (probe summary line 29; `apiKeySource` none in every `init`, and a `credential_expires_at` on every signed-in `result` after an `init`), and they did not stall: in the runs stopped at their `init`, it came within about half a second of the start, and in the runs stopped at their first reply text, that text came about two seconds after the start (mcp-plain, mcp-var-only, mcp-strict-only and mcp-lane summary line 5; sigterm and sigint summary line 5). So a dialog that blocked the read is unlikely for that launch. But their parent was a Claude Code session's shell, not Instafy Desktop, and only a person watching a turn that Desktop starts can settle it (B.4).
- **Item 14, auto memory: partly checked.** It writes: in `memory-write`, two writes under the person's Claude Code project folder for that folder, a note and its index, succeeded without a prompt (memory-write lines 37 and 53, 88 and 92), the `result` lists no denial (line 101), and nothing changed in the folder. Every `init` names that memory folder as `memory_paths.auto`, under the throwaway configuration folder when one was set (signed-out line 1). Whether it loads, the model did not show: `memory-read` answered with no tool call, and the word it was asked for sat only in the note, not in the index. But `memory-read`'s first request carried more input than `memory-write`'s first request in the same folder, though its prompt was shorter (memory-write line 3 against memory-read line 3), which fits the index loading at the start of the session.

What every `init` also showed, outside the 14 items:
- **Tools that reach beyond the turn, and a messaging socket.** Every lane `init` listed `ListAgents`, `SendMessage`, `RemoteTrigger`, `PushNotification`, `CronCreate`, `ScheduleWakeup` and `Workflow`, and named a messaging socket of its own process; the signed-out run lacked only `RemoteTrigger` and `PushNotification` (signed-out line 1). No run sent or received a message. What a reading of 2.1.325's code says they do is in 1.2, which records them as open.
- **What the signed-out run lacked.** Every signed-in `init` listed skills, an agent and tools that the signed-out run, with an empty configuration folder, did not (isolation line 1 against signed-out line 1), and the signed-out run's `init` listed the `WebFetch` tool, which no signed-in `init` did. That run changed the sign-in and the configuration folder together, so that the sign-in brings them is an inference (3.3). Only Claude Code's four built-in plugins loaded in any run, and no plugin the person installed (3.3, 7.4).
- **`init` is not the final list.** In `baseline` a `commands_changed` line followed the `init` (baseline line 3), so a check of the `init` describes only the start.
- **The run log.** On 2.1.325 a successful `result` carries a second API-price estimate (`model_usage_since_last_result`, with `costUSD` and `costBasis`) and `credential_expires_at`, and each `rate_limit_event` the plan's usage and overage state (baseline lines 12-13). The run log as first built kept all of them; 3.6 has what it keeps now.
- **Paths named by slug.** `init`'s `memory_paths.auto` (memory-write line 1), a command that read the memory index (line 60) and a background command's notice of its output file (bash-end line 31) name the folder by Claude Code's slug of its absolute path, which the redactor as first built did not match; 3.6 has the redaction now.

### B.3 What Phase 0 changed in this design

- **3.6, 3.7 and 2.6.** The end-of-turn rule and the `result` the reply comes from (item 5), with what later readings of 2.1.325's code added to it: tasks a subagent started, and subagents that end while such a task runs; tool steps that end the turn; notifications folded into the running turn, which the lane does not try to detect, so their tasks stay awaited; the follow-up wait, 15 s, restarted after other turns in between at most five times; `claude`'s own exit, which during that wait ends the turn as the wait would when it is 0, or 1 after a last `result` that was an error; a Monitor watch's event turn, which never gives the reply, while the follow-up of a task that any turn began can; turns held for the next, whose `result`s never give the reply; the launch check of a later `init`, by its `cwd`, read through its longest existing ancestor when it no longer exists, and its `original_cwd`; usage summed and permission denials combined over a turn's results; what the run log keeps, from every line; the redaction of slugged paths.
- **2.7 and A.2.** A resume miss is read from its structured `result` before `init`, whatever the exit status (item 7).
- **3.3, 1.2, 4.2 and 7.4.** Claude Code's auto memory is written outside the folder without asking (items 11 and 14), which the consent text does not mention; item 11's precondition, whose directories the consent text does not mention either; the skills, agent and tools that the signed-out run lacked; the conflict between setting `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_AUTO_MEMORY` and AGENTS.md's rule.
- **1.2, 3.6 and 4.4.** Every Bash command runs in a session and process group of its own (item 9).
- **1.2, 2.8, 3.5, 3.9 and D8.** The cross-session and account tools and the messaging socket, recorded as open, with a queued prompt's two routes and a durable `CronCreate`'s write under the folder's `.claude/`.
- **3.2, 3.5, 3.9, 7.5, 9, D5, D30 and D-J.** The results of items 1, 2, 4 and 10.
- **8.** The fake binary's scenarios and the tests of the behaviour above.

### B.4 Open before phase 8

The check left these open, to settle before phase 8 turns the lane on. Phase 8 needs items 2, 3 and 5 of section 9's list to hold: the second to fifth entries bear on items 2 and 3, and the ninth to eleventh on item 5.

1. **The version.** Settled on 2026-11-19: the maintainer chose 2.1.325, the version Phase 0 ran on. The floor, `MIN_SUPPORTED_VERSION` (3.2), and `CLAUDE_CODE_LANE_VERIFIED_THROUGH` rose from 2.1.314 to it together in one reviewed change, with the tests and fixtures that pinned 2.1.314 and the floor's text in 1.2, 3.2 and A.2. So the items phase 8 gates on need no second run on 2.1.314. Raising `CLAUDE_CODE_LANE_VERIFIED_THROUGH` alone would have marked versions nobody ran as verified.
2. **Item 2's `claude-md-and-agents-md` variant**, with Project instructions set to it in the person's own settings, as section 9 asks, and in a space with `AGENTS.md` and no `CLAUDE.md` as well as one with both.
3. **Positive controls for item 2.** A run of the planted folder with the project and local sources on, which must show each channel firing (the hooks' markers, the planted settings, skill, agent and command, the memory tokens), so that the lane run's silence counts for each channel that fired; and a comparison of the first request's input with an unplanted twin folder, so that the memory verdict does not rest on what the model lists. The live suite's own item 2 and item 12 checks have no such control either.
4. **Item 3's other servers: the person's own and plugin servers.** Control runs with a throwaway `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, which prints an `init` without a sign-in: one with a server at the person's own scope, and one with a plugin whose `.mcp.json` starts a marker command. The run without the strict flags must list each server and leave its marker before the lane run's empty list counts. The live suite's item 3 check needs the same controls.
5. **Files loaded on demand below the root.** Memory files and `.claude/` content in a subfolder and in an untracked dependency folder, a turn that reads one file there with no other tool, and a search of its reply, its `commands_changed` lines and the markers for their tokens, with a control.
6. **Item 13, the keychain.** A person watches the first lane turn that Desktop starts and records whether macOS shows a keychain or privacy prompt that names Instafy.
7. **What the messaging socket and the cross-session tools accept**, checked by hand on the maintainer's own Mac, with no agent running `claude`: whether a message from another of their sessions reaches a lane-flag run, sent once while a tool runs and once after the turn's `result`, which of 1.2's two routes 2.1.325 takes for each, mid-turn into the person's turn or a `peer` turn of its own, and how its stream shows it; whether a lane-flag run can message, list or schedule, and where a `CronCreate` call under the lane's flags writes, the folder's `.claude/scheduled_tasks.json` included; and which of these `--permission-prompts none` declines. The owner then accepts these routes in 1.2 or restricts the tools (D8).
8. **What the consent text says a turn changes without asking** (4.2): that a turn, steered or not, can write Claude Code's notes for the folder, which later sessions there load, and that it edits files in any directory the person's own settings add as it does in the space's folder. Phase 8's wording review settles both wordings (section 9). The consent record keeps no version of the text, so they should be decided before anyone consents. Drafted on 2026-11-18: the text names both (4.2), and the review settles their wording.
9. **Item 5 on a real `claude`, with this build.** Before any id goes into `CLAUDE_CODE_LANE_USER_IDS`, a person runs the live suite's item 5 by hand, on 2.1.325, the floor since entry 1 was settled (section 9, phase 8). It needs no tool beyond the suite: it feeds this build's adapter as `claude` runs, and stops `claude` where the lane would, after the follow-up wait too. The run's verdict is the result, not a favourable timing order: the suite's FAILS stands, since another run would not make it hold, and its UNCONFIRMED settles nothing. B.2 then records whether item 5's end-of-turn rule as now built holds on a real `claude`. The runbook cannot stand in for it: its own reading of the lane's rule is the rule as first built, and replaying the output of its step 6 and step 7's `bash-wait` through this build's adapter would need a replay harness, which the repository does not have. The suite's own loop asks the same follow-up wait helper as runtime-agent's supervisor, `supervise::FollowUpWait`, with the same 15 s grace and five restarts, after each line and on each tick, but it is not the supervisor: until a lane turn runs through runtime-agent on a real `claude`, the supervisor's own reading loop rests on the supervision tests (8).
10. **A subagent that its own command or watch resumes.** By a reading of 2.1.325's code, no subagent is parked under `-p`: 2.1.325 parks a subagent, or runs a forked skill in the background, only in an interactive session, so a background subagent's own notification is queued at the end of each of its runs. A subagent's own background command or Monitor watch still keeps it resumable: when the subagent's run ends while one runs, its report reaches the main conversation at once, marked as possibly interim, and the command's or watch's notification later resumes it as a new run, whose answer comes in a follow-up of its own (3.6). The lane waits for no command or watch, so when nothing else holds the turn it ends at that report, and the answer never comes. No run showed it. A run by hand, with a background subagent that starts a background command and returns before the command ends, shows what 2.1.325 prints, and whether the lane should hold the turn while a command or watch that one of its subagents started still runs.
11. **A notice a resumed session queues at its start.** By a reading of 2.1.325's code, `--resume` queues at startup notices about the previous session's background tasks that left no record of their end, and runs each as a turn with no model request whose `result` reads as a held turn's that names no run (3.6). By the same reading, such notices run before the person's own turn. The adapter ends no turn, and starts no follow-up wait, before the person's own `result`, so they only delay the turn (3.6). Whether a lane turn that ends while a background command it started still runs, as a dev server does, leaves such a task for the next lane turn is not settled. A run by hand of two lane-flag turns in one session, with no agent running `claude`, the first stopped while a background command it started still runs and the second resuming that session, shows what 2.1.325 prints.
